I haven't really thought about it like that. It's just that there's always something in the way now. \n\nLike the other morning, I went into the kitchen and the coffee maker was just left on, burning the dregs at the bottom of the glass. It's one of those commercial Bunn brewers, like they have in diners? Brews a whole pot in three minutes flat. Fantastic machine. I actually found it at a garage sale a couple years ago. The guy wanted twenty bucks, but I talked him down to twelve. He had this whole driveway full of old restaurant equipment because his brother used to run a diner off Interstate 80. That was right before they built that huge bypass. Man, what a nightmare that construction project was. Took them like four years, and the traffic was backed up past the county line every single afternoon. I used to have to take the dirt roads behind the state park just to get into town... \n\nAnyway, sorry. What were we talking about? Being angry? I guess I just don't really know how to measure it, to be honest with you.\n\nDoctor: Are there things or activities you've stopped doing because they feel overwhelming or too stressful?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. Work stuff, mostly. Like, I completely missed this important filing date for my contractor license recently. Which is crazy because I always used to have that circled in red on the calendar. But they actually moved the licensing office down to that new municipal building on 4th street. You know the one? They tore down the old Starlight bowling alley to build it. Man, I spent half my twenties in that bowling alley. They had this league on Thursday nights, dollar pitchers of beer, and the best chili-cheese fries you've ever had. The guy who ran the shoe rental counter, Jimmy, he had this glass eye that always looked like it was staring right at the ceiling... \n\nBut, uh, anyway. Because I missed that, and just because I'm not keeping up the way I used to, I've had to turn down or delay a bunch of small repair jobs. It just feels like too much to handle right now. \n\nAnd at home, it's... well, it's putting a lot of real tension into my marriage. It's just getting harder to follow through on regular family responsibilities. Like, I was supposed to take my wife's car in for new brake pads last week and I just couldn't get myself to do it. It's a Honda CR-V. Great gas mileage on those things, but the factory tires they put on them wear out so fast. I always tell people to switch to Michelins as soon as they can. You get what you pay for with rubber, you know? My buddy bought these cheap knock-offs online once and had a blowout on the interstate... \n\nWait, what was the question again? Overwhelming? Yeah, just... I'm just not keeping up with my jobs or my family stuff.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding certain places or situations because of how they make you feel?\n\nPatient: Avoiding places? I mean, not really. I don't think so. I still go out when I have to. Like, I went down to the hardware store just yesterday. The local one on Main Street, not the big box places. They have this old golden retriever that just sleeps right in the middle of aisle four. Name's Buster. You have to literally step over him to get to the plumbing supplies. \n\nWhich, by the way, are getting ridiculously expensive. I was just looking for a simple PVC P-trap for the guest bathroom sink because it's been dripping a little. Water damage is no joke, you know? My cousin had a tiny leak behind his fridge a few years back and it ruined his whole subfloor. He had to rip up real hardwood. Red oak, I think it was. Beautiful grain. You can't even get wood like that anymore without paying an arm and a leg, mostly because all the independent lumber yards around here got bought out by that one corporate supplier from out of state. They came in and just slashed all the prices until the local guys went under, and then jacked them right back up... \n\nBut, uh, sorry. What were we talking about? Avoiding situations? No, I don't think I'm doing that. I'm just not doing much of anything, really.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Sad? I don't... I wouldn't say sad, exactly. Not like crying or anything. Just kind of... flat, I guess? Like someone let the air out of the tires. \n\nSpeaking of tires, I actually did end up checking the pressure on my truck yesterday. It's an old Ford F-150. Got over two hundred thousand miles on it and the engine still purrs. They really don't build them like they used to, you know? The new ones have so many computer chips in them, you can't even change your own oil without a diagnostic scanner. My neighbor bought one of those electric trucks last year. The thing is dead silent. It's actually kind of dangerous because you don't even hear it coming down the street. He almost backed into the mail carrier's truck last Tuesday. Which would have been a disaster because our mail carrier, Dave, he's been doing this route for fifteen years and he's retiring next month. They're having a little block party for him. His wife makes these incredible lemon bars...\n\nWait, sorry. Sad? No, I don't think I'm sad. Just... yeah, just flat.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel disconnected from your emotions or like you're just going through the motions of daily life?\n\nPatient: Disconnected? I mean, yeah, going through the motions sounds about right. It's like I'm just on autopilot lately. Wake up, drink coffee, stare at the wall... \n\nI did try a new coffee blend the other day, actually. Usually I just get the cheap stuff in the big plastic tub, but they had this local roast on sale at the grocery store. It was supposed to have notes of caramel and toasted pecan, but honestly, it just tasted burnt to me. It's funny how they market that stuff, you know? Like wine. My brother-in-law is super into wine now. He went to Napa Valley last year and came back acting like a sommelier. He bought this special temperature-controlled fridge just for his bottles. Takes up half his dining room. Which is crazy because his house isn't even that big, it's one of those split-levels they built a million of in the seventies. The foundation on those things is always settling weird. You can put a marble on his kitchen floor and it'll roll right into the hallway...\n\nBut, uh, what did you ask? Disconnected? Yeah, I guess I just feel like I'm not really present. Avoiding things? Yeah, no, I'm not really avoiding anything specific. I guess that's basically what we were just talking about, right? The... the muted feeling. Yeah, I don't know, I just don't feel much of anything very strongly right now. It's like the volume is turned down on everything. \n\nWhich is funny, because I used to be the guy who felt everything at maximum volume. Like when my brother got married, I bawled like a baby during his vows. He married this girl he met at a dog park, actually. They kept bumping into each other because their dogs kept getting their leashes tangled up. She had this little French bulldog that snored louder than a grown man. I don't get the appeal of those smushed-face dogs, honestly. They always sound like they're struggling to breathe. Give me a working breed any day. We had a German Shepherd when I was a kid, named Duke. Smartest dog I ever met. He figured out how to open the latch on the back gate with his nose. My dad had to install a padlock just to keep him in the yard. My dad was pretty handy like that, always fixing things around the house instead of calling a repairman. He built this incredible treehouse for us out of scrap lumber he got from a construction site down the road. It had a rope ladder and a trapdoor and everything. We used to spend entire summers up there, reading comic books and eating freeze pops... \n\nWait, sorry. The emotions thing. Yeah, I don't know. I just don't feel things very intensely right now. It's all just kind of flat.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Weight? No, I don't think so. I haven't really noticed any big changes with my weight. My clothes still fit the same. \n\nThough I did have to buy some new jeans a couple of years ago because the dryer shrank my old ones. Or at least, that's what I tell myself. But honestly, those modern washing machines, they just don't make them like they used to. My aunt had this old top-loading Maytag that lasted her thirty years. Thirty years! And it never broke down once. She used to do laundry every Saturday morning, regular as clockwork. She'd hang all the sheets out on the clothesline in the backyard, even in the winter. Said the cold air made them smell fresher. I don't know about that, but they were always stiff as a board when she brought them back inside. She had this huge willow tree in her backyard, too. We used to swing from the branches when we were kids. My cousin fell out of it once and broke his arm in two places. Had to wear this giant plaster cast for six weeks right in the middle of summer. He was miserable. Couldn't even go swimming in the community pool. That pool was the best, though. They had a diving board and a concession stand that sold those little paper cups of Italian ice. I always got the cherry flavor, even though it stained your mouth red for a week...\n\nBut, uh, what did you ask me? Weight loss? Right, no. I haven't lost any weight that I'm aware of.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities or situations that you used to enjoy, but now avoid because they feel too difficult or stressful to deal with?\n\nPatient: Activities I used to enjoy but now avoid? I mean, I guess I'm not really avoiding anything specific because it's too hard. I'm just... not doing them. Like, I used to love fishing. \n\nI haven't been out to the lake in months. Usually, I'd go with my buddy Mark. Mark's got this old aluminum boat, a real classic, you know? It's got a few dents in the side from when he misjudged the dock at the marina. That marina is always so crowded on the weekends anyway. You get all these folks with their fancy speedboats blaring music, not even caring about the people trying to fish in peace. Mark always brings a cooler full of sandwiches, too. His wife makes them—she uses this spicy mustard that clears your sinuses right out. She works at a deli downtown, actually. The kind of place where they slice the pastrami so thin you can see through it. I always meant to ask her how they get the slicer so sharp. I tried sharpening my kitchen knives at home once with one of those pull-through gadgets, but I think I just made them worse. Couldn't even cut a tomato without squishing it. Tomatoes are tricky anyway, you really need a serrated edge. My grandmother used to grow these massive beefsteak tomatoes in her backyard. The vines would take over the whole trellis. She’d make these incredible BLTs in the summer...\n\nBut yeah, no. I'm not avoiding fishing or anything else because it's stressful. I just haven't felt like going.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from activities that you normally would enjoy?\n\nPatient: Pleasure from things I enjoy? Well, like I was saying about the fishing... I just don't feel like doing much. Even when I do try to do something I usually like, it's just kind of... there. No real enjoyment in it. \n\nLike, I tried watching the game last Sunday. Usually, I'm glued to the TV, you know? Yelling at the refs, the whole nine yards. I have this lucky jersey I always wear, an old Brett Favre one. The numbers are peeling off the back from washing it so many times. I probably shouldn't put it in the dryer, but who has time to air dry clothes? Besides, hanging things on a drying rack takes up so much space in the living room. My apartment isn't that big to begin with. The layout is just weird. The kitchen is basically a hallway, and the fridge opens right into the oven handle. It’s a nightmare if you’re trying to cook a big meal. Not that I cook big meals much anyway. I usually just throw a frozen pizza in or something. Those frozen pizzas have gotten so expensive lately, though. Used to be you could get three for ten bucks, now it's like seven dollars for one. And the crust is never right. It's either soggy in the middle or hard as a rock on the edges. I read somewhere you're supposed to bake them directly on the rack, but then the cheese drips down and burns on the bottom of the oven, and that smell takes days to get out...\n\nWait, what was the question? Enjoying things? Yeah, I just... I don't get any real joy out of anything right now. Even the game was just noise on the TV.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're depressed most of the time?\n\nPatient: Depressed most of the time? I mean... I don't know if I'd use the word depressed. I just feel kind of flat, you know? Just going through the motions. \n\nLike yesterday, I was standing in line at the grocery store. Just standing there holding a basket with, like, milk and some bread. And the line was taking forever because the cashier had to do a price check on a head of lettuce. Who does a price check on lettuce? It's like a dollar. But the manager had to come over, and he was wearing this ridiculous tie with little golf clubs on it. It reminded me of my old boss at the hardware store. He used to wear these crazy novelty ties every Friday. Said it boosted morale. I don't know how a tie with singing fish on it is supposed to make anyone want to sell more power tools, but whatever. He was a decent guy, though. Let me take time off when my dog got sick. Poor Buster, he ate a whole pack of sugar-free gum that fell out of my coat pocket. Xylitol, the vet said. Toxic to dogs. Had to pump his stomach and keep him overnight on an IV. Cost me a fortune, but what are you gonna do? They're family. After that, I started keeping all my gum in the glove compartment of my car. Except in the summer, it all melts together into one giant minty blob. You try to peel a piece off and you just get sticky foil all over your fingers. Then your steering wheel gets sticky, and... \n\nSorry, what were we talking about? Depressed? Yeah, I don't know. I'm just not feeling like myself lately. Just tired and... flat.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to feel emotions, such as happiness or sadness, as intensely as you used to?\n\nPatient: Unable to feel emotions? I don't think it's that I can't feel them at all, it's just... I guess maybe they aren't as strong? Like, things that used to make me really happy just kind of get a \"huh, that's nice\" reaction now. \n\nTake my sister's kids, for example. She's got these twin boys, absolute terrors but usually they crack me up. Last week they were over, and they decided it would be a great idea to build a fort out of the couch cushions. Which is fine, except they used my good throw blankets for the roof. The ones my aunt knitted for me before she moved to Florida. She moved down to Sarasota, actually. Said she couldn't take another Midwest winter. I don't blame her, the snow here gets ridiculous. Remember that blizzard we had back in, what, 2014? The one where they shut down the interstate for three days? I was stuck at my apartment with nothing but a half-empty box of cereal and some questionable milk. I tried making a snowman on my balcony, but the wind just blew it all over the sliding glass door. Took me hours to scrape the ice off the glass once it froze over. You have to use one of those plastic scrapers, though, because if you use metal, you'll scratch the glass. My neighbor scratched his windshield to hell using a metal spatula once. A spatula! Who uses a spatula on a car? I guess if you're desperate...\n\nBut anyway, the twins. They made this huge mess, and usually I'd either be laughing my head off or getting a little annoyed that they knocked over my lamp. But I just sort of looked at them and went back to my room. Didn't feel much of anything, really. Just... tired.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like nothing can make you feel excited or happy anymore?\n\nPatient: Excited or happy? I mean, I guess it's not that *nothing* can, I just... I haven't really felt that way in a while. It's hard to get worked up about anything. \n\nLike, I got this email the other day about a class reunion. My 25th, actually. Wait, no, it would be my 30th. Man, time flies. Anyway, they're renting out this big banquet hall downtown. The one by the river, with the big chandeliers. I went to a wedding there once, years ago. My buddy Dave's wedding. They had this open bar, which was a terrible idea because Dave's uncle got completely hammered and tried to do the worm on the dance floor. Knocked over a whole tray of champagne glasses. The catering staff was furious. You know, catering has got to be a tough job. You're constantly on your feet, carrying heavy trays, dealing with people who complain that the chicken is too dry. And wedding food is always dry, isn't it? Unless you get the beef, but even then it's a toss-up. I always try to eat a little something before I go to a wedding, just in case. Usually just a sandwich or something. You can never go wrong with a good turkey sandwich. But you have to have the right mayo. None of that sweet stuff, it has to be real mayonnaise. My mom used to make these sandwiches for our road trips...\n\nWhat was I saying? Oh, right, the reunion. Yeah, I saw the email and usually I'd be at least a little curious to see who's going, maybe excited to catch up with a few people. But I just looked at it and deleted it. I just didn't care. There's just no spark right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to cry or feel emotional pain when something sad happens?\n\nPatient: Unable to cry? I don't... I don't know if I'm unable to. I just haven't really had anything happen that made me want to cry, I guess? Or maybe I just haven't noticed. \n\nI did watch this movie the other night, though. One of those old black-and-white ones they play on cable at two in the morning. I can never sleep past 2 AM lately, so I end up watching whatever's on. Anyway, it was this war movie, and there was this scene where the guy's best friend gets shot. And usually, that kind of stuff gets to me. I'm a sucker for a good drama. My ex-wife used to tease me because I teared up during that one commercial with the polar bears. You know the one? Where the little cub gets separated from its mom on the ice? They used to play it every Christmas. Christmas commercials are always so manipulative, though. They just want you to buy soda or cars. Who buys a car for Christmas? Like, you wake up and there's a Lexus in the driveway with a giant red bow on it. Where do you even get a bow that big? Do they sell them at the dealership, or do you have to go to some specialty giant-bow store? And what do you do with it afterward? You can't just throw it in the recycling bin. It would take up the whole thing. The garbage men in my neighborhood are really strict about that. If the lid isn't completely closed, they just leave the bin there. Last month, I had a piece of cardboard sticking out, and they just slapped a sticker on it and drove right past. I had to wait a whole week...\n\nWait, what was the question? Crying? Yeah, I don't know. During that movie, I just sort of watched the guy die and didn't really feel much. Just changed the channel. So maybe? I'm not really sure.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Traumatic events? No, I don't think so. I mean, nothing like a car crash or being in a war or anything like that. My life's been pretty quiet, honestly. 

Well, except for the time the water main broke on my street. That was a nightmare, but I wouldn't call it traumatic, just a massive headache. It was right in the middle of July, hottest week of the year. The city took three days to fix it. Three days without running water! You don't realize how much you use the sink until you turn the handle and nothing comes out. I had to go buy those big gallon jugs of spring water just to flush the toilet. And the grocery store was completely sold out of the regular ones, so I had to buy the expensive alkaline water. Who drinks alkaline water anyway? It tastes exactly the same, but they charge you three times as much because it has a higher pH or whatever. It's a total scam. Just like those air purifiers they try to sell you on infomercials. I bought one once, thinking it would help with my allergies. Turns out it was just a glorified fan with a flimsy little filter. It didn't do a thing. I tried to return it, but the company had this ridiculous 30-day policy, and by the time I figured out it was garbage, it was day 32. Customer service wouldn't budge. I spent forty-five minutes on hold listening to this awful elevator music. It was that really slow jazz, with the saxophone that just keeps repeating the same four notes... 

But yeah, no trauma. Just the usual everyday stuff, I guess. Nothing major.

But they actually moved the licensing office down to that new municipal building on 4th street. You know the one? They tore down the old Starlight bowling alley to build it. Man, I spent half my twenties in that bowling alley. They had this league on Thursday nights, dollar pitchers of beer, and the best chili-cheese fries you've ever had. The guy who ran the shoe rental counter, Jimmy, he had this glass eye that always looked like it was staring right at the ceiling... \n\nBut, uh, anyway. Because I missed that, and just because I'm not keeping up the way I used to, I've had to turn down or delay a bunch of small repair jobs. It just feels like too much to handle right now. \n\nAnd at home, it's... well, it's putting a lot of real tension into my marriage. Yeah, just... I'm just not keeping up with my jobs or my family stuff.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding certain places or situations because of how they make you feel?\n\nPatient: Avoiding places? I mean, not really. I don't think so. I still go out when I have to. Like, I went down to the hardware store just yesterday. The local one on Main Street, not the big box places. They have this old golden retriever that just sleeps right in the middle of aisle four. Name's Buster. You have to literally step over him to get to the plumbing supplies. \n\nWhich, by the way, are getting ridiculously expensive. They came in and just slashed all the prices until the local guys went under, and then jacked them right back up... \n\nBut, uh, sorry. What were we talking about? Avoiding situations? Got over two hundred thousand miles on it and the engine still purrs. They really don't build them like they used to, you know? It's actually kind of dangerous because you don't even hear it coming down the street. He almost backed into the mail carrier's truck last Tuesday. Takes up half his dining room. Which is crazy because his house isn't even that big, it's one of those split-levels they built a million of in the seventies. It would take up the whole thing. The garbage men in my neighborhood are really strict about that. If the lid isn't completely closed, they just leave the bin there. Last month, I had a piece of cardboard sticking out, and they just slapped a sticker on it and drove right past. I had to wait a whole week...\n\nWait, what was the question? Crying? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Major life changes? Yeah, I guess you could say that. My father-in-law moved in with us recently. He brought so much stuff with him, too. We had to rent one of those big box trucks. Have you seen how much they charge for those now? They advertise nineteen-ninety-nine a day on the side of the truck, but then they get you with the mileage and the insurance. Plus you have to fill up the gas tank before you bring it back, and the gas station right next to the rental place always jacks up their prices because they know you're desperate. I ended up driving three miles down the road just to save ten cents a gallon. It's the principle of the thing, you know? 

Just like those grocery store loyalty cards. They track everything you buy just to give you a coupon for fifty cents off paper towels. I read an article once about how they use algorithms to figure out your whole life based on what kind of vitamins or lotion you buy. It’s creepy. Privacy is just completely dead, isn't it? Everybody has a camera on their doorbell now. You can't even walk down the street without ending up on someone's security footage. My neighbor has one that actually talks to you if you get too close to his driveway. It says, "You are currently being recorded." Scared the absolute life out of me the first time it happened. I was walking by early in the morning and I almost dropped my tackle box...

Which, I guess, gets back to why I'm here. Because I haven't even been able to enjoy fishing lately. Ever since my father-in-law moved in, I just can't seem to get my head quiet, even when I'm trying to fish or when I'm out in my workshop. I've been snapping at my wife over stupid little house things, just constantly on edge. And I'm putting off all these jobs and paperwork I used to stay right on top of. I don't know, my brain just won't shut off.

I mean, I guess I'm not really avoiding anything specific because it's too hard. I'm just... not doing them. Like, I used to love fishing. \n\nI haven't been out to the lake in months. Usually, I'd go with my buddy Mark. Mark's got this old aluminum boat, a real classic, you know? It's got a few dents in the side from when he misjudged the dock at the marina. That marina is always so crowded on the weekends anyway. You get all these folks with their fancy speedboats blaring music, not even caring about the people trying to fish in peace. Mark always brings a cooler full of sandwiches, too. His wife makes them—she uses this spicy mustard that clears your sinuses right out. She works at a deli downtown, actually. The kind of place where they slice the pastrami so thin you can see through it. I always meant to ask her how they get the slicer so sharp. I tried sharpening my kitchen knives at home once with one of those pull-through gadgets, but I think I just made them worse. Couldn't even cut a tomato without squishing it. Tomatoes are tricky anyway, you really need a serrated edge. My grandmother used to grow these massive beefsteak tomatoes in her backyard. The vines would take over the whole trellis. She’d make these incredible BLTs in the summer...\n\nBut yeah, no. I'm not avoiding fishing or anything else because it's stressful. I just haven't felt like going.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from activities that you normally would enjoy?\n\nPatient: Pleasure from things I enjoy? Well, like I was saying about the fishing... I just don't feel like doing much. Even when I do try to do something I usually like, it's just kind of... there. No real enjoyment in it. \n\nLike, I tried watching the game last Sunday. Usually, I'm glued to the TV, you know? Yelling at the refs, the whole nine yards. I have this lucky jersey I always wear, an old Brett Favre one. The numbers are peeling off the back from washing it so many times. I probably shouldn't put it in the dryer, but who has time to air dry clothes? Besides, hanging things on a drying rack takes up so much space in the living room. My apartment isn't that big to begin with. The layout is just weird. The kitchen is basically a hallway, and the fridge opens right into the oven handle. It’s a nightmare if you’re trying to cook a big meal. Not that I cook big meals much anyway. I usually just throw a frozen pizza in or something. Those frozen pizzas have gotten so expensive lately, though. Used to be you could get three for ten bucks, now it's like seven dollars for one. And the crust is never right. It's either soggy in the middle or hard as a rock on the edges. I read somewhere you're supposed to bake them directly on the rack, but then the cheese drips down and burns on the bottom of the oven, and that smell takes days to get out...\n\nWait, what was the question? Enjoying things? Yeah, I just... I don't get any real joy out of anything right now. Even the game was just noise on the TV.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're depressed most of the time?\n\nPatient: Depressed most of the time? I mean... I don't know if I'd use the word depressed. I just feel kind of flat, you know? Just going through the motions. \n\nLike yesterday, I was standing in line at the grocery store. Just standing there holding a basket with, like, milk and some bread. And the line was taking forever because the cashier had to do a price check on a head of lettuce. Who does a price check on lettuce? It's like a dollar. But the manager had to come over, and he was wearing this ridiculous tie with little golf clubs on it. It reminded me of my old boss at the hardware store. He used to wear these crazy novelty ties every Friday. Said it boosted morale. I don't know how a tie with singing fish on it is supposed to make anyone want to sell more power tools, but whatever. He was a decent guy, though. Let me take time off when my dog got sick. Poor Buster, he ate a whole pack of sugar-free gum that fell out of my coat pocket. Xylitol, the vet said. Toxic to dogs. Had to pump his stomach and keep him overnight on an IV. Cost me a fortune, but what are you gonna do? They're family. After that, I started keeping all my gum in the glove compartment of my car. Except in the summer, it all melts together into one giant minty blob. You try to peel a piece off and you just get sticky foil all over your fingers. Then your steering wheel gets sticky, and... \n\nSorry, what were we talking about? Depressed? Yeah, I don't know. I'm just not feeling like myself lately. Just tired and... flat.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to feel emotions, such as happiness or sadness, as intensely as you used to?\n\nPatient: Unable to feel emotions? I don't think it's that I can't feel them at all, it's just... I guess maybe they aren't as strong? Like, things that used to make me really happy just kind of get a \"huh, that's nice\" reaction now. \n\nTake my sister's kids, for example. She's got these twin boys, absolute terrors but usually they crack me up. Last week they were over, and they decided it would be a great idea to build a fort out of the couch cushions. Which is fine, except they used my good throw blankets for the roof. The ones my aunt knitted for me before she moved to Florida. She moved down to Sarasota, actually. Said she couldn't take another Midwest winter. I don't blame her, the snow here gets ridiculous. Remember that blizzard we had back in, what, 2014? The one where they shut down the interstate for three days? I was stuck at my apartment with nothing but a half-empty box of cereal and some questionable milk. I tried making a snowman on my balcony, but the wind just blew it all over the sliding glass door. Took me hours to scrape the ice off the glass once it froze over. You have to use one of those plastic scrapers, though, because if you use metal, you'll scratch the glass. My neighbor scratched his windshield to hell using a metal spatula once. A spatula! Who uses a spatula on a car? I guess if you're desperate...\n\nBut anyway, the twins. They made this huge mess, and usually I'd either be laughing my head off or getting a little annoyed that they knocked over my lamp. But I just sort of looked at them and went back to my room. Didn't feel much of anything, really. Just... tired.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like nothing can make you feel excited or happy anymore?\n\nPatient: Excited or happy? I mean, I guess it's not that *nothing* can, I just... I haven't really felt that way in a while. It's hard to get worked up about anything. \n\nLike, I got this email the other day about a class reunion. My 25th, actually. Wait, no, it would be my 30th. Man, time flies. Anyway, they're renting out this big banquet hall downtown. The one by the river, with the big chandeliers. I went to a wedding there once, years ago. My buddy Dave's wedding. They had this open bar, which was a terrible idea because Dave's uncle got completely hammered and tried to do the worm on the dance floor. Knocked over a whole tray of champagne glasses. The catering staff was furious. You know, catering has got to be a tough job. You're constantly on your feet, carrying heavy trays, dealing with people who complain that the chicken is too dry. And wedding food is always dry, isn't it? Unless you get the beef, but even then it's a toss-up. I always try to eat a little something before I go to a wedding, just in case. Usually just a sandwich or something. You can never go wrong with a good turkey sandwich. But you have to have the right mayo. None of that sweet stuff, it has to be real mayonnaise. My mom used to make these sandwiches for our road trips...\n\nWhat was I saying? Oh, right, the reunion. There's just no spark right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to cry or feel emotional pain when something sad happens?\n\nPatient: Unable to cry? I don't... I don't know if I'm unable to. I just haven't really had anything happen that made me want to cry, I guess? Or maybe I just haven't noticed. \n\nI did watch this movie the other night, though. One of those old black-and-white ones they play on cable at two in the morning. I can never sleep past 2 AM lately, so I end up watching whatever's on. Anyway, it was this war movie, and there was this scene where the guy's best friend gets shot. And usually, that kind of stuff gets to me. I'm a sucker for a good drama. My ex-wife used to tease me because I teared up during that one commercial with the polar bears. You know the one? Where the little cub gets separated from its mom on the ice? They used to play it every Christmas. Christmas commercials are always so manipulative, though. They just want you to buy soda or cars. Who buys a car for Christmas? Like, you wake up and there's a Lexus in the driveway with a giant red bow on it. Where do you even get a bow that big? Do they sell them at the dealership, or do you have to go to some specialty giant-bow store? And what do you do with it afterward? You can't just throw it in the recycling bin. It would take up the whole thing. The garbage men in my neighborhood are really strict about that. If the lid isn't completely closed, they just leave the bin there. Yeah, I don't know. During that movie, I just sort of watched the guy die and didn't really feel much. Just changed the channel. So maybe? I'm not really sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any traumatic events in your life?\n\nPatient: Traumatic events? No, I don't think so. I mean, nothing like a car crash or being in a war or anything like that. My life's been pretty quiet, honestly. \n\nWell, except for the time the water main broke on my street. But honestly, those modern washing machines, they just don't make them like they used to. My aunt had this old top-loading Maytag that lasted her thirty years. Thirty years! And it never broke down once. She used to do laundry every Saturday morning, regular as clockwork. She'd hang all the sheets out on the clothesline in the backyard, even in the winter. Said the cold air made them smell fresher. I don't know about that, but they were always stiff as a board when she brought them back inside. She had this huge willow tree in her backyard, too. We used to swing from the branches when we were kids. My cousin fell out of it once and broke his arm in two places. Had to wear this giant plaster cast for six weeks right in the middle of summer. He was miserable. Couldn't even go swimming in the community pool. That pool was the best, though. They had a diving board and a concession stand that sold those little paper cups of Italian ice. I always got the cherry flavor, even though it stained your mouth red for a week...\n\nBut, uh, what did you ask me? Weight loss? Right, no. I haven't lost any weight that I'm aware of.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities or situations that you used to enjoy, but now avoid because they feel too difficult or stressful to deal with?\n\nPatient: Activities I used to enjoy but now avoid? I mean, I guess I'm not really avoiding anything specific because it's too hard. I'm just... not doing them. Like, I used to love fishing. \n\nI haven't been out to the lake in months. Usually, I'd go with my buddy Mark. Mark's got this old aluminum boat, a real classic, you know? It's got a few dents in the side from when he misjudged the dock at the marina. That marina is always so crowded on the weekends anyway. You get all these folks with their fancy speedboats blaring music, not even caring about the people trying to fish in peace. Mark always brings a cooler full of sandwiches, too. His wife makes them—she uses this spicy mustard that clears your sinuses right out. She works at a deli downtown, actually. The kind of place where they slice the pastrami so thin you can see through it. I always meant to ask her how they get the slicer so sharp. I tried sharpening my kitchen knives at home once with one of those pull-through gadgets, but I think I just made them worse. Couldn't even cut a tomato without squishing it. Tomatoes are tricky anyway, you really need a serrated edge. My grandmother used to grow these massive beefsteak tomatoes in her backyard. The vines would take over the whole trellis. She’d make these incredible BLTs in the summer...\n\nBut yeah, no. I'm not avoiding fishing or anything else because it's stressful. I just haven't felt like going.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from activities that you normally would enjoy?\n\nPatient: Pleasure from things I enjoy? Well, like I was saying about the fishing... I just don't feel like doing much. Even when I do try to do something I usually like, it's just kind of... there. No real enjoyment in it. \n\nLike, I tried watching the game last Sunday. Usually, I'm glued to the TV, you know? Yelling at the refs, the whole nine yards. I have this lucky jersey I always wear, an old Brett Favre one. The numbers are peeling off the back from washing it so many times. I probably shouldn't put it in the dryer, but who has time to air dry clothes? Besides, hanging things on a drying rack takes up so much space in the living room. My apartment isn't that big to begin with. The layout is just weird. The kitchen is basically a hallway, and the fridge opens right into the oven handle. It’s a nightmare if you’re trying to cook a big meal. Not that I cook big meals much anyway. I usually just throw a frozen pizza in or something. Those frozen pizzas have gotten so expensive lately, though. Used to be you could get three for ten bucks, now it's like seven dollars for one. And the crust is never right. It's either soggy in the middle or hard as a rock on the edges. I read somewhere you're supposed to bake them directly on the rack, but then the cheese drips down and burns on the bottom of the oven, and that smell takes days to get out...\n\nWait, what was the question? Enjoying things? Yeah, I just... I don't get any real joy out of anything right now. Even the game was just noise on the TV.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're depressed most of the time?\n\nPatient: Depressed most of the time? I mean... I don't know if I'd use the word depressed. I just feel kind of flat, you know? Just going through the motions. \n\nLike yesterday, I was standing in line at the grocery store. Just standing there holding a basket with, like, milk and some bread. And the line was taking forever because the cashier had to do a price check on a head of lettuce. Who does a price check on lettuce? It's like a dollar. But the manager had to come over, and he was wearing this ridiculous tie with little golf clubs on it. It reminded me of my old boss at the hardware store. He used to wear these crazy novelty ties every Friday. Said it boosted morale. I don't know how a tie with singing fish on it is supposed to make anyone want to sell more power tools, but whatever. He was a decent guy, though. Let me take time off when my dog got sick. Poor Buster, he ate a whole pack of sugar-free gum that fell out of my coat pocket. Xylitol, the vet said. Toxic to dogs. Had to pump his stomach and keep him overnight on an IV. Cost me a fortune, but what are you gonna do? They're family. After that, I started keeping all my gum in the glove compartment of my car. Except in the summer, it all melts together into one giant minty blob. You try to peel a piece off and you just get sticky foil all over your fingers. Then your steering wheel gets sticky, and... \n\nSorry, what were we talking about? Depressed? Yeah, I don't know. I'm just not feeling like myself lately. Just tired and... flat.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to feel emotions, such as happiness or sadness, as intensely as you used to?\n\nPatient: Unable to feel emotions? I don't think it's that I can't feel them at all, it's just... I guess maybe they aren't as strong? Like, things that used to make me really happy just kind of get a \"huh, that's nice\" reaction now. \n\nTake my sister's kids, for example. She's got these twin boys, absolute terrors but usually they crack me up. Last week they were over, and they decided it would be a great idea to build a fort out of the couch cushions. Which is fine, except they used my good throw blankets for the roof. The ones my aunt knitted for me before she moved to Florida. She moved down to Sarasota, actually. Said she couldn't take another Midwest winter. I don't blame her, the snow here gets ridiculous. Remember that blizzard we had back in, what, 2014? The one where they shut down the interstate for three days? I was stuck at my apartment with nothing but a half-empty box of cereal and some questionable milk. I tried making a snowman on my balcony, but the wind just blew it all over the sliding glass door. Took me hours to scrape the ice off the glass once it froze over. You have to use one of those plastic scrapers, though, because if you use metal, you'll scratch the glass. My neighbor scratched his windshield to hell using a metal spatula once. A spatula! Who uses a spatula on a car? I guess if you're desperate...\n\nBut anyway, the twins. They made this huge mess, and usually I'd either be laughing my head off or getting a little annoyed that they knocked over my lamp. But I just sort of looked at them and went back to my room. Didn't feel much of anything, really. Just... tired.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like nothing can make you feel excited or happy anymore?\n\nPatient: Excited or happy? I mean, I guess it's not that *nothing* can, I just... I haven't really felt that way in a while. It's hard to get worked up about anything. \n\nLike, I got this email the other day about a class reunion. My 25th, actually. Wait, no, it would be my 30th. Man, time flies. Anyway, they're renting out this big banquet hall downtown. The one by the river, with the big chandeliers. I went to a wedding there once, years ago. My buddy Dave's wedding. They had this open bar, which was a terrible idea because Dave's uncle got completely hammered and tried to do the worm on the dance floor. Knocked over a whole tray of champagne glasses. The catering staff was furious. You know, catering has got to be a tough job. You're constantly on your feet, carrying heavy trays, dealing with people who complain that the chicken is too dry. And wedding food is always dry, isn't it? Unless you get the beef, but even then it's a toss-up. I always try to eat a little something before I go to a wedding, just in case. Usually just a sandwich or something. You can never go wrong with a good turkey sandwich. But you have to have the right mayo. None of that sweet stuff, it has to be real mayonnaise. My mom used to make these sandwiches for our road trips...\n\nWhat was I saying? Oh, right, the reunion. Yeah, I saw the email and usually I'd be at least a little curious to see who's going, maybe excited to catch up with a few people. But I just looked at it and deleted it. I just didn't care. There's just no spark right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to cry or feel emotional pain when something sad happens?\n\nPatient: Unable to cry? I don't... I don't know if I'm unable to. I just haven't really had anything happen that made me want to cry, I guess? Or maybe I just haven't noticed. \n\nI did watch this movie the other night, though. One of those old black-and-white ones they play on cable at two in the morning. I can never sleep past 2 AM lately, so I end up watching whatever's on. Anyway, it was this war movie, and there was this scene where the guy's best friend gets shot. And usually, that kind of stuff gets to me. I'm a sucker for a good drama. My ex-wife used to tease me because I teared up during that one commercial with the polar bears. You know the one? Where the little cub gets separated from its mom on the ice? They used to play it every Christmas. Christmas commercials are always so manipulative, though. They just want you to buy soda or cars. Who buys a car for Christmas? Like, you wake up and there's a Lexus in the driveway with a giant red bow on it. Where do you even get a bow that big? Do they sell them at the dealership, or do you have to go to some specialty giant-bow store? And what do you do with it afterward? You can't just throw it in the recycling bin. It would take up the whole thing. The garbage men in my neighborhood are really strict about that. If the lid isn't completely closed, they just leave the bin there. Last month, I had a piece of cardboard sticking out, and they just slapped a sticker on it and drove right past. I had to wait a whole week...\n\nWait, what was the question? Crying? Yeah, I don't know. During that movie, I just sort of watched the guy die and didn't really feel much. Just changed the channel. So maybe? I'm not really sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any traumatic events in your life?\n\nPatient: Traumatic events? No, I don't think so. I mean, nothing like a car crash or being in a war or anything like that. My life's been pretty quiet, honestly. \n\nWell, except for the time the water main broke on my street. That was a nightmare, but I wouldn't call it traumatic, just a massive headache. It was right in the middle of July, hottest week of the year. The city took three days to fix it. Three days without running water! You don't realize how much you use the sink until you turn the handle and nothing comes out. I had to go buy those big gallon jugs of spring water just to flush the toilet. And the grocery store was completely sold out of the regular ones, so I had to buy the expensive alkaline water. Who drinks alkaline water anyway? It tastes exactly the same, but they charge you three times as much because it has a higher pH or whatever. It's a total scam. Just like those air purifiers they try to sell you on infomercials. I bought one once, thinking it would help with my allergies. Turns out it was just a glorified fan with a flimsy little filter. It didn't do a thing. I tried to return it, but the company had this ridiculous 30-day policy, and by the time I figured out it was garbage, it was day 32. Customer service wouldn't budge. I spent forty-five minutes on hold listening to this awful elevator music. It was that really slow jazz, with the saxophone that just keeps repeating the same four notes... \n\nBut yeah, no trauma. Just the usual everyday stuff, I guess. Nothing major.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant losses or major life changes recently?\n\nPatient: Major life changes? Yeah, I guess you could say that. My father-in-law moved in with us recently. He brought so much stuff with him, too. We had to rent one of those big box trucks. Have you seen how much they charge for those now? They advertise nineteen-ninety-nine a day on the side of the truck, but then they get you with the mileage and the insurance. Plus you have to fill up the gas tank before you bring it back, and the gas station right next to the rental place always jacks up their prices because they know you're desperate. I ended up driving three miles down the road just to save ten cents a gallon. It's the principle of the thing, you know? \n\nJust like those grocery store loyalty cards. They track everything you buy just to give you a coupon for fifty cents off paper towels. I read an article once about how they use algorithms to figure out your whole life based on what kind of vitamins or lotion you buy. It’s creepy. Privacy is just completely dead, isn't it? Everybody has a camera on their doorbell now. You can't even walk down the street without ending up on someone's security footage. My neighbor has one that actually talks to you if you get too close to his driveway. It says, \"You are currently being recorded.\" Scared the absolute life out of me the first time it happened. I was walking by early in the morning and I almost dropped my tackle box...\n\nWhich, I guess, gets back to why I'm here. Because I haven't even been able to enjoy fishing lately. Ever since my father-in-law moved in, I just can't seem to get my head quiet, even when I'm trying to fish or when I'm out in my workshop. I've been snapping at my wife over stupid little house things, just constantly on edge. And I'm putting off all these jobs and paperwork I used to stay right on top of. I don't know, my brain just won't shut off."
  }
